134	### Severity threshold tiers (warn / fail / critical) (4 tasks — 4/4 ✅)
135	> **Day 1 design decision: severity weights — ✅ settled in [ADR 0005](adr/0005-severity-tier-weights.md) (warn 0.5 / fail 1.0 / critical 2.0; health = 100×(1−Σpenalty/(N×2.0))).**
136	- [x] ✅ Add `warn_threshold`, `fail_threshold`, `critical_threshold` fields to check model — nullable `Numeric` columns on `Check` (NULL → plain pass/fail) — migration `9c59b6a44f33`
137	- [x] ✅ Alembic migration — threshold columns + `status` enum (`pass`, `warn`, `fail`, `critical`) **+ the monitor-kind / metric columns below (one migration)** — the one-shot Week-3 schema seam `9c59b6a44f33` (tested up→down→up; `results.status` retargeted from `passed/failed/skipped` with a data-update-before-CHECK-swap; `run_service` now writes `pass`/`fail` binary-fallback per ADR 0005). `alembic check` clean (no model drift)
138	- [x] ✅ Post-processing in GX result handler — derive `warn` / `fail` / `critical` from observed value (PR-C) — `services/severity.py` (`extract_metric` + `derive_status`), wired into `run_service._build_result`. Thresholds band the GX **unexpected-%** as `metric_value` (higher=worse, ordered, unset-tier skipped); thresholds-as-policy override GX `success`; binary fallback when no thresholds / no metric. **Settled in [ADR 0016](adr/0016-severity-derivation-semantics.md)** (incl. A→B reversibility: raw `observed_value` retained → switch is additive `direction` column + backfill, never destructive). `duration_ms` stays NULL (per-check timing not separable from GX's suite-level `validate()`). 16 unit + 1 integration test; both modules 100%
139	- [x] ✅ Update check CRUD + run result response schemas with threshold fields + status values — check-CRUD thresholds (PR-B2) + result response now carries `status` (`pass`/`warn`/`fail`/`critical`) + `metric_value` (probe `CheckResultResponse`, PR-C)
140	
141	### Monitor abstraction & metric storage — do-now seams (3 tasks — 3/3 ✅)
142	> **Day 1 design decision: `check.kind` discriminator + numeric metric storage — ✅ settled in [ADR 0012](adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md); rides the same migration.** Keeps v1.x auto-monitors (freshness / volume / schema-drift / anomaly — post-v1 Theme A) from forcing a check/result schema rewrite. v1 implements `expectation` only.
143	- [x] ✅ Add `kind` discriminator to check model (`'expectation'` default; `freshness`/`volume`/`schema_drift`/`anomaly` reserved) — `checks.kind` `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'expectation'` + CHECK over the 6 reserved kinds (incl. `comparison`, ADR 0014) — migration `9c59b6a44f33`
144	- [x] ✅ Generalise run path to dispatch by `check.kind` (`expectation` → GX `CheckRunner`; others raise `NotImplementedError`) — PR-D: `run_service._specs_for_checks` dispatches by kind; a non-`expectation` check raises `NotImplementedError` → the run goes terminal `failed` **without invoking the adapter** (never silently run as a GX expectation). Composes with the Week-5 `connection.type` runner selection (`kind` picks the monitor, type picks the adapter). `run_service` 100%; test fixtures now set `kind` to mirror DB rows
145	- [x] ✅ Add `metric_value` (NUMERIC) + `duration_ms` (INT) to results — SQL-aggregatable metric for Week-6 trends + v1.1 anomaly; per-check runtime for cost surface — nullable columns on `Result` — migration `9c59b6a44f33`
146	
147	### Column profiler (3 tasks — 3/3 ✅)
148	- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (Snowflake) — nulls, distinct count, min / max, top values — `profile_service` + `POST /suites/{id}/profile` (require_permission **edit**, suite-scoped so the connection is access-gated). Reads-only, persists nothing: one aggregate query (row count + null/distinct/min/max per column) + one top-N-values query per column, then `assemble_profile`. **SQL-injection-safe** — queries are built with the **SQLAlchemy Core expression language** (`select`/`table`/`column`, dialect-quoted) so there's no raw-string SQL sink (no S608/B608/CodeQL `py/sql-injection`); identifiers are additionally allowlist-validated (`validate_identifier`, strict `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*$`) as defence-in-depth + a clean early 422, and `top_n` is `int()`-coerced. v1 → 422: non-Snowflake type (dispatch generalises Week 5), bad identifier, no schema; execution failure → 502 (adapter exception never echoed). min/max/top-values NaN-sanitised. 28 tests (22 pure: identifier allowlist incl. injection strings, compiled-SQL builders, assembly, div-by-zero, NaN; 6 endpoint via fake conn: stats, injection-422, unsupported-type-422, 502, edit-gated, no-schema-422); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 90% (live `_open_connection` is the deferred warehouse seam)
149	- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (ADLS / S3) — same stats via Pandas on sampled file — extended `profile_service` into a type dispatcher (`profile_connection`): SQL types → in-warehouse aggregation (unchanged), flat-file types (`adls_gen2`/`s3`) → download a **sample** of the file into Pandas and compute the same stats (`profile_dataframe`). Same `POST /suites/{id}/profile` endpoint, now polymorphic: SQL targets pass `table`/`schema`, flat-file targets pass `path` (+ optional `file_format`, else inferred from extension); response carries whichever identity applies. CSV + Parquet. **pandas "load less data" levers** (per the user's scale.html prompt): column projection (CSV `usecols`, Parquet `columns=` via pyarrow schema) so profiling 3 of N columns doesn't read all N, + row sampling (`_SAMPLE_ROWS`), + **zero-copy Arrow-backed dtypes** on the Parquet read (`dtype_backend="pyarrow"` — parquet is already Arrow, kept zero-copy; the stat helpers + `_to_native` are Arrow-scalar/`pd.NA`-safe). Stats are vectorised pandas (no Python row loops — enhancingperf N/A; CoW is a no-op for a read-only profiler + a process-global flag, so not toggled; pyarrow CSV engine rejected — it's incompatible with `nrows`/callable-`usecols`). NaN/Timestamp→JSON-safe. v1 → 422: unsupported type (UC still pending), missing target, unknown format, missing column; read failure → 502 (exception never echoed). Reuses `ColumnProfile`/`ProfileResult`. 24 new tests (pure `profile_dataframe`/`infer_file_format`/`_to_native`/projection-parse + endpoint via fake `_read_dataframe`); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 85% (only the live `_open_connection`/`_download_bytes` network seams uncovered — deferred smoke). _Deferred: streaming/range reads (whole object still downloaded before sampling) + out-of-core (Dask) — future work if profiling cost bites._
150	- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (Unity Catalog) — via Databricks SQL Warehouse — added `unity_catalog` to the profiler's **SQL** branch (reuses `profile_table` + the SQLAlchemy Core query builders + `assemble_profile` — no duplicate aggregation logic). `_open_connection` now dispatches the engine by type (`_engine_args`): Snowflake URL or a `REDACTED` URL via the installed `databricks` SQLAlchemy dialect. UC's **3-level namespace** (`catalog.schema.table`) is handled by building the table with a `quoted_name(f"{catalog}.{schema}", quote=False)` so the dialect emits three dotted parts rather than quoting the dotted string as one — verified by compiling the real builders against the Databricks dialect. Injection-safe: every part (catalog/schema/table/column) is allowlist-validated (no raw-string SQL, no CodeQL py/sql-injection surface). Endpoint gained `catalog` (request + response); the dispatcher requires it for UC (`422 profile_target_invalid` if absent). 12 new tests (catalog-aware builders compiled to SQL, `_engine_args` URL dispatch, UC endpoint stats via the same fake conn, missing-catalog 422, bad-catalog-identifier 422); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 86% (only the live `_open_connection`/`_download_bytes` network seams uncovered — deferred smoke). **Completes the column profiler across all 4 datasources.**
151	
152	### Flat file check specifics (2 tasks — 2/2 ✅)
153	- [x] ✅ Check types for flat files: schema validation, row count, null checks, ~~freshness by filename date~~ — `FlatFileCheckRunner` (`datasources/flatfile.py`): downloads the file (S3/ADLS) into a pandas DataFrame and runs the suite's GX expectations against GX's **pandas DataFrame asset**, so all GX expectation-kind checks (schema/row-count/null/value checks) run against flat files. Extracted the shared GX machinery — snake_case→GX-class translation, GX-result→`SuiteOutcome` mapping, and the suite/validation-definition `run_expectations` flow — into `datasources/gx_runner.py` (reused by the Snowflake runner and the future UC runner; no duplication). Flat-file IO (`download_bytes` + full-file `read_dataframe`) moved to `flatfile.py` behind primitives (raw config + resolved secret, not the ORM — matches `build_snowflake_runner`), and the column profiler now shares it. Because GX runs **in-process on the DataFrame**, the run path is fully unit-tested with a canned frame (real GX execution, not mocked) — only the network download is the deferred-smoke seam. 16 new tests; `gx_runner` 100%, `flatfile` 76% (only `download_bytes` uncovered). _Connection-type → runner dispatch (Snowflake vs flat-file) is the Week-5 `run_suite` wiring; **freshness-by-filename** is the reserved `freshness` monitor kind (deferred, ADR 0012), not a GX expectation._
154	- [x] ✅ Batch resolution — resolve batching regex to matched files, pick latest or specific batch — `resolve_batch` (`datasources/flatfile.py`): a batch `pattern` is a regex whose **first capture group is the batch key**; `latest` selects the greatest key (lexicographic — ISO dates sort chronologically), falling back to the storage modified-time when the pattern has no group; `specific` selects the file whose key equals a given `batch`. `BatchNotFoundError` on no match / unknown batch. The filter+select logic is pure and fully unit-tested (8 tests); object listing (`list_files` — S3 `list_objects_v2` paginated / ADLS `list_blobs`) is the live seam, and `resolve_batch_file` composes list+resolve. _Wiring a check's target to a resolved batch path is the Week-5 `run_suite` dispatch._
155	
156	### Unity Catalog check specifics (2 tasks — 2/2 ✅)
157	- [x] ✅ UC table check path — UC table → GX DataFrame datasource → run suite — `UnityCatalogCheckRunner` (`datasources/unity_catalog.py`): reads the target table from the Databricks SQL Warehouse into a pandas DataFrame (`pd.read_sql_table` via the databricks SQLAlchemy dialect — reflection quotes identifiers, so no hand-built SQL) and runs the suite's GX expectations against GX's **pandas DataFrame asset** — the "GX DataFrame datasource" shape (§5), the same shape Databricks Labs DQX consumes, so v1.1 swaps GX→DQX behind this one interface. Reuses the shared `gx_runner` (`run_expectations` + result mapping) exactly like the flat-file runner; the `catalog` is pinned in the connection URL (`build_databricks_url`, now shared with the column profiler — no duplication) so the 2-level `schema.table` resolves to `catalog.schema.table`. Because GX runs **in-process on the DataFrame**, the run path is unit-tested with a canned frame (real GX execution); only the reflect+read (`_read_table`) is the deferred-smoke seam. `build_unity_catalog_runner` mirrors `build_snowflake_runner` (raw config + resolved PAT, not the ORM). 14 tests; `unity_catalog` 91% (only `_read_table` uncovered). _Connection-type → runner dispatch is the Week-5 `run_suite` wiring._

249	## Week 6 — Results dashboard + alerting
250	
251	**Exit gate:** Full results dashboard live across all source types; alerts firing with suppression.

253	### Results dashboard (11 tasks — 2/11 ✅)
254	> **Build as an in-app React page** (the GX-Cloud-style redesign Phase C), **not Grafana** — reading Postgres directly would bypass DataQ's per-suite sharing + PII redaction; Grafana is deferred to an optional post-v1 ops add-on ([ADR 0018](adr/0018-results-surface-and-grafana-deferral.md), accepted). The Week-5 run-enablement backend it gates on is **done**: `runs.py` read endpoints (`GET /runs`, `GET /runs/{id}`, `GET /pipeline_runs`) + `POST /suites/{id}/run` (PR-C0b); [#215](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/215) target (PR-C0a). The `results.metric_value`/`duration_ms` columns were seam-built for these trend charts (ADR 0012).
255	- [x] ✅ **Results page scaffold** (PR-C1) — the in-app `/results` page + sidebar nav (Connections · Suites · **Results** · Profile). **Runs tab**: a runs table (suite name, status, triggered-by, started, duration) with a status filter, each row opening a **run-detail drawer** that drills into the per-check results (check name + expectation, severity tag, `metric_value`, observed value). **Pipeline runs tab**: the orchestration monitoring feed (`pipeline_runs`) with a provider filter. Pure presentation helpers (`resultsFormat.ts` — duration/timestamp formatters, status→colour maps) are unit-tested; `api/runs.ts` mirrors the C0b schemas. Demo seed (`demo_data.py`) now lands runs + results (pass/pass/warn/fail spread) + two pipeline-runs + suite targets so the page has real content. 10 vitest + 2 Playwright (`results.spec.ts`). _The rich widgets below build on this scaffold._

  "content": "## Week 6 · Phase 3 — Results page enrichment\n\nBuilds the two Results-page tasks of Week 6 Phase 3 (ADR [0022](docs/adr/0022-week6-prototype-adoption-and-chart-library.md)) on top of the dashboard/run-detail track (PR #333) — internal label **PR-C2**.\n\n### 3.1 — Results filter bar expansion (`4f7c81b`)\nExpands the runs-tab filter bar beyond the status filter shipped in PR-C1:\n- **Suite** (searchable, lists all accessible suites), **Environment** (dev/qa/uat/prod), **Datasource** (Snowflake / Flat file / Unity Catalog), **Date** (preset windows 24h/7d/30d).\n- A run only carries `suite_id`, so env/datasource are joined via each suite's connection (the tab now also fetches `listConnections`).\n- New reusable `DATASOURCE_CATEGORY` map in `api/connections.ts` collapses ADLS+S3 into one **Flat file** category — covers the separate *Datasource type filter* roadmap task.\n- **Date filter uses preset windows, not a true `RangePicker`** — antd v6 doesn't bundle dayjs and it isn't installed, so a range picker would add a dependency. Matches the dashboard's existing window control. Pure `isWithinWindowDays` helper, unit-tested.\n\n### 3.2 — Orchestration panel polish (`4996b7a`)\n- **30s auto-poll** on the Pipeline-runs tab (refreshes both pipeline runs and DQ runs; `reload()` keeps rows visible across the refetch — no spinner flash).\n- **DQ-run correlation** — new `DQ run` column joins each monitored pipeline run to the run(s) it triggered via the `triggered_by` marker `<provider>:<pipeline_or_dag_id>:<provider_run_id>` (verified against `orchestration_service._trigger_suites`; one pipeline run → many DQ runs, one per binding). Status tags deep-link to `/results/:runId`. Pure `pipelineRunMarker` helper kept in sync with the backend.\n\n### Docs (`5314f9a`)\nprogress.md was stale at 2/27 — it never recorded PR #333. This syncs Week 6 to **13/27** (dashboard widgets, run-detail route, CSV/JSON export, nav/shell, plus this PR's filters/orchestration) and updates the snapshot + aggregate table. Epic #175 synced to match.\n\n### Testing\n- `pnpm lint` / `pnpm typecheck` clean; **258/258** vitest green.\n- New: 4 runs-filter tests (suite/env/datasource/date) + pipeline-run→DQ-run correlation test + `isWithinWindowDays` / `pipelineRunMarker` unit tests.\n- One functionality per commit; each manually verifiable in the running app.\n\n### Notes / follow-ups\n- Failed-check **sample rows** stay withheld (PII, [#226](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/226)) — unchanged here.\n- PDF export remains deferred to backlog (CSV/JSON shipped in #333).\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"

id: toolu_01YHHjaETpJsE1N9qGG9e6zG
```
AntD `Tabs` with `items` lazy-mounts inactive tab panels by default (only the active tab renders), so the two tabs' `listRuns` calls won't fire simultaneously on load — but they still re-fetch independently when switching tabs (no shared cache). The double-fetch is real but lower cost than if both mounted at once.

Here are my findings.

```json
[
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 274,
    "summary": "PipelineRunsTab polls with setInterval — the exact anti-pattern the codebase deliberately avoids. LiveRunProgress.tsx:94 documents using a self-scheduling setTimeout 'not setInterval, so a slow request can't pile up overlapping fetches.' This new code uses setInterval AND fires two independent reloads (reload + reloadRuns) every tick.",
    "failure_scenario": "If listPipelineRuns or listRuns takes longer than 30s (slow API / large 200-row payload / network stall), setInterval keeps firing new fetch pairs while prior ones are in flight — overlapping requests pile up and stale resolutions can clobber newer state. The existing self-scheduling-timeout pattern (already in LiveRunProgress) prevents exactly this; it should be extracted into a shared hook and reused rather than reintroducing the rejected approach."
  },
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 41,
    "summary": "DATE_WINDOWS duplicates Dashboard.tsx's RANGES (lines 28-32): both define the same 24h/7d/30d trailing-window presets with near-identical labels ('Last 24h', 'Last 7 days'/'7d', 'Last 30 days'/'30d'). Two parallel window-preset definitions that will drift.",
    "failure_scenario": "Adding/changing a window (e.g. a 90d option, or relabeling) requires editing both lists; they already disagree on labels ('7d' vs 'Last 7 days'), so the UI is inconsistent between the dashboard and results surfaces. A single shared WINDOW_PRESETS const (value→days→label) in resultsFormat.ts or a shared module would cover both."
  },
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 101,
    "summary": "listRuns({ limit: 200 }) is fetched independently in both RunsTab (line 101) and PipelineRunsTab (line 267) with no shared cache. Same 200-row payload, two fetchers.",
    "failure_scenario": "Switching from the Runs tab to the Pipeline runs tab re-fetches the full 200-run list from scratch (AntD Tabs lazy-mounts, so it's a fresh useAsyncData each time), and PipelineRunsTab then re-fetches it again every 30s. The run list is downloaded redundantly across the two tabs; lifting the runs fetch to the parent Results component and passing it down would fetch once."
  },
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 184,
    "summary": "Five near-identical Filter+Select blocks (Status/Suite/Environment/Datasource/Date, lines ~184-238) — each is the same Filter wrapper + Select with value/onChange/style/options, varying only in the options array and width. Copy-paste with slight variation.",
    "failure_scenario": "Each new filter or cross-cutting change (e.g. add allowClear, a consistent width, a 'reset filters' affordance, disabled-while-loading) must be applied five times by hand, and the five widths (150/220/130/160/150) are already ad-hoc. A small data-driven FilterSelect helper or a config array mapped over would collapse the repetition and keep the controls uniform."
  },
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 144,
    "summary": "RunsTab renders the filtered runs list as a plain expression (`const runs = state.data.filter(...)`) that re-runs the full multi-predicate filter over up to 200 rows on every render, while the upstream suiteMeta/suiteOptions are memoized. The hot-path filter is not memoized.",
    "failure_scenario": "Every keystroke in the searchable Suite Select (and every unrelated re-render) re-evaluates the 5-predicate filter — including a Map lookup and Date math per row via isWithinWindowDays — over the whole 200-row page. Minor at 200 rows but inconsistent with the surrounding useMemo discipline; wrapping it in useMemo keyed on the filter state + data would match the rest of the component."
  }
]

I now have a complete picture. Let me finalize my findings.

The most concrete, real issues:

1. **Filter-state staleness during connections load** — When a user sets the env or datasource filter, then the page reloads/remounts, there's a window where `connectionsState` is loading and `suiteMeta` has null env/category. But since filter state resets to 'all' on mount, this only bites if filter state were persisted. Not a real bug. Discard.

2. **PipelineRunsTab swallows `runsState` error** — real but graceful degradation. Medium-low.

3. **200-row cap means triggered DQ runs / filtered runs can be silently missing.** Real data-completeness issue but it's an existing design pattern, documented.

4. **`runsByMarker` keyed by full `triggered_by`** — markers like `manual:<uid>`, `schedule:<id>`, `probe:<uid>` also get added to the map. They'll never match a `pipelineRunMarker` output (which is always `provider:pipeline:runid`), so no false correlation. Correct. Good — no bug.

Let me double check one genuine concern: the date filter `Number(dateWindow)` when `dateWindow` is e.g. `'1'` → 1. Fine. `'all'` handled separately. Good.

Let me verify whether there's a stale-closure issue with `pollMs` default and the interval — no, it's in deps.

One real subtle issue: in `RunsTab`, the env/datasource filters exclude runs whose suite's connection is **inaccessible to the current user** (meta null). A run can be visible (suite shared) but its connection not shared. Applying any env/datasource filter then hides legitimately-visible runs. This is a real UX correctness issue worth flagging.

Let me also reconsider: `suiteMeta.get(r.suite_id)` returns undefined if the suite isn't in the accessible suites list (a run for a suite the user lost access to but still has runs). Then `meta?.env` is undefined; with env filter active, excluded. Fine/expected.

I reviewed the three frontend files line-by-line plus the enclosing components, the `useAsyncData` hook, the runs/connections/suites API types, and the backend `triggered_by` marker format. Verified-correct (no findings): `pipelineRunMarker` ordering matches backend `{provider}:{pipeline_or_dag_id}:{provider_run_id}`; `runsByMarker` cannot false-match `manual:`/`schedule:`/`probe:` markers; `isWithinWindowDays` boundary and `started_at ?? created_at` fallback are sound (queued runs keep `created_at`); the setInterval effect deps are stable (`reload` is `useCallback([])`) so no interval pile-up or stale closure; `RUN_STATUS_COLORS` covers all `RunStatus` keys; React keys use `r.id`. The four findings above are the real candidates, ordered by confidence — the top two (inaccessible-connection filter exclusion, swallowed `runsState` error) are the strongest.

The filter then does `if (env !== 'all' && meta?.env !== env) return false` — so during the connections load window, or for any run whose suite connection the user can't read, applying an Env/Datasource filter hides those runs entirely rather than showing them as unknown. Summary: 4 findings, all real. The two strongest are altitude issues — the `setInterval` one-off that contradicts the repo's documented self-scheduling-poll pattern (`LiveRunProgress.tsx`), and the duplicated full-runs fetch + client-side reconstruction of the backend `triggered_by` correlation contract against a capped 200-row page (correlation belongs in the pipeline-runs API). The `pipelineRunMarker` brittle-contract-duplication and the env/category filter dropping rows during load round it out.

On conventions (CLAUDE.md §4/§11): the datasource-category map handles ADF/Airflow correctly — `adf: null, airflow: null` with a doc comment that orchestration types "never back a suite/run," and the filter dropdown is built only from `DATASOURCE_CATEGORIES` (no ADF/Airflow option). No §4/§11 datasource-vs-orchestration violation. No Azure lock-in or other quotable CLAUDE.md rule breaches in the diff.

`reload` is `useCallback`-stable, so the `useEffect` interval deps `[reload, reloadRuns, pollMs]` won't churn. The interval is correctly cleaned up. Good.

One last check: in PipelineRunsTab, the fetcher passed to `useAsyncData` is `() => listPipelineRuns(...)` — an inline arrow recreated each render. The comment at line 39 says "the fetcher identity..." Let me confirm the effect doesn't depend on fetcher identity (it depends on nonce). Confirmed from line 39-44: re-runs on nonce, fetcher identity intentionally not a dep. Stable.

The removed-behavior audit came back clean — status filtering is preserved (now folded into a combined predicate), the empty-state message is intentionally reworded ("No runs match these filters." is arguably better than "No runs yet." since filters now exist, though it loses the true-empty distinction), suiteNames→suiteMeta preserves the id→name fallback, and the old PipelineRunsTab behavior is fully retained plus the new DQ-run column. Cross-file tracing confirmed: all new exports (`DATASOURCE_CATEGORY*`, `DatasourceCategory`, `isWithinWindowDays`, `pipelineRunMarker`) resolve, `pipelineRunMarker` exactly matches the backend marker format (`orchestration_service._trigger_suites:197`), no circular import (runs.ts imports only `./client`), and `tsc --noEmit` passes clean. The only real concern is finding #1 — the new env/category filters silently empty the table if the connections join data fails to load, with no error surfaced. Finding #2 is an efficiency nit.
